# P6a Undo and Sync Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Editors undo and redo their own changes (also changes still waiting to be saved), two people editing one tree no longer lose edits on a 409 (the store rebases instead of reloading), unsent edits survive a reload or a Google sign-in, a tab never sends another account's queue, and every member can read the change history, where editors revert one change and the Manager reverts the tree to a point.

**Architecture:**
- **The queue becomes a list of entries.** `pending` holds `PendingEntry { id, op, reverts?, revertsToSeq? }`. The local `id` links an entry to its undo and redo steps and to an undo of it that is queued behind it; `reverts` becomes the request's `revertsSeq` once the original has a seq. Pure helpers in `features/tree/pending.ts` replay entries on a tree and report what was kept, what was dropped (with why) and the inverse of every kept entry.
- **Rebase, not reload.** On `409 VERSION_CONFLICT` the store reads `GET /ops?since=` page by page (`fetchOpsSince`), and the pure `rebase()` (`features/tree/rebase.ts`) applies the missed ops to the confirmed tree, recognises an op of ours the server had already saved (a lost answer), replays the rest and drops what no longer applies. The third conflict in a row, a gap in the log, or an answer that cannot be read still reloads the whole tree (the P5 path).
- **Undo is just another op.** The undo stack holds `{ op, inverse, ref }` for this user's changes in this session. Undo applies the inverse locally and queues it with `reverts: ref`; redo applies the original again. Every place where the queue changes (a save, a refusal, a rebase, a reload) also updates both stacks: saved entries are named by seq, dropped ones leave both stacks, kept ones get the inverse they have on the new tree.
- **Identity and persistence.** The store learns the account from the app shell (`setAccount`). Every `POST /ops` carries `expectedUserId`; a new middleware answers `IDENTITY_MISMATCH` (409) before the clan-role check, and the store stops and keeps the queue. The queue is mirrored to `sessionStorage` (`features/tree/persist.ts`) and restored for the same account, through the rebase when the tree moved on.
- **History.** A new endpoint `GET /api/clans/:id/history?before=&limit=` lists operations newest first with the author's display name and the stored `inverse`. The new `features/history` page renders plain-language summaries (`describeOp`, shared with the undo badge), "Revert" sends one stored inverse with `revertsSeq`, and "Revert to this point" (Owner) builds one flattened batch of inverses sent with `revertsToSeq`. Reverts go through the tree store, so they are undo steps like any edit.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 6, Zod 4, fast-check 4, Express 5, Mongoose 9.12 (`MongoMemoryReplSet` in tests), React 19, React Router 8, TanStack Query 5, Zustand 5.1, @xyflow/react 12.12, i18next, Vitest 5, Testing Library, Playwright 1.65 with @axe-core/playwright.

**Spec:** [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-11-ancestors-tree-design.md`](../specs/2026-11-11-ancestors-tree-design.md) §6.1 (request fields, limits, rules), §6.3 (rebase and its P6a details, other errors), §6.4 (undo, redo, history, "Revert to this point"), §7.3 (store, guard, `sessionStorage`), §7.5 (toolbar), §7.8 (keyboard), §13.1 (`IDENTITY_MISMATCH`). Roadmap: [`2026-11-12-roadmap.md`](2026-11-12-roadmap.md), "P6 — Editing UX" (the P6a split and the items it takes). Ledger: `.superpowers/sdd/p5-final-review.md`, section "Ledger triage".

**Decisions made with the user for P6a**
1. Undo and redo stacks hold only this user's ops in this session and are cleared on reload. Undo sends the locally computed inverse as a new op with `revertsSeq`; for a pending op, `revertsSeq` is filled in once the original is saved. An inverse that no longer applies is not sent: "Could not undo: <reason>", and the entry leaves the stack. A rebase (or refusal) that drops an op removes its undo and redo entries.
2. The rebase follows §6.3 and pages through `/ops?since=` until caught up. After 3 consecutive conflicts the store falls back to the P5 reload.
3. The `sessionStorage` queue is keyed by user, clan and confirmed version: same user, clan and version → restored; newer version → restored through the rebase; another account → dropped with `discarded`; unreadable → dropped with a notice.
4. `expectedUserId` on every `POST /ops`. A mismatch is 409 `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, never retried: the store stops, keeps every op and shows a notice.
5. History page `/clans/:id/history`: every member sees it; newest first, paged; the server joins author names; plain-language summaries in vi and en; "Revert" for editors and up; "Revert to this point" for the Owner only, as one flattened batch of at most 500 inverses sent with `revertsToSeq`, which the server accepts only from the Owner; a confirmation with the count; all or nothing; one undo step.
6. Unsent edits are never lost silently: keep them, ask before an action that would lose them, and tell the user when they are lost anyway.

**Decisions made while planning** (the controller reports them to the user)
- **A new history endpoint, not a wider `/ops`.** `GET /ops?since=` stays lean for the rebase (oldest first, no names). `GET /history?before=&limit=` is newest first, joins `authorName` and returns the stored `inverse`, `revertsSeq` and `revertsToSeq`. Pages hold 50 entries by default, at most 100.
- **The third conflict in a row reloads.** §6.3 says both "retry at most 3 times" and "after 3 conflicts in a row … reload". The plan follows the second: conflicts 1 and 2 rebase, conflict 3 reloads. Task 14 aligns the spec text.
- **A lost answer is recognised.** When the fetched ops contain, under this account, exactly the op at the head of the queue (compared with the new `sameOp`, which ignores key order), that op was saved before its answer was lost. It is confirmed with that seq instead of replayed (which would duplicate it or fail with a misleading notice). The same check runs when a restored queue meets a newer tree.
- **"Revert to this point" is never rebased.** It is built from the log up to one version. The store refuses it (`OUT_OF_DATE`) while anything is pending or when its tree is at another version, and a rebase that finds someone else's op drops it with the `rejected` notice (the reason reads "The family tree was just updated by someone else").
- **Redo sends no `revertsSeq`.** Redo is the undone change made again; the history shows it as a normal change. The undo stack keeps the latest 100 steps.
- **Unions per person are capped at 100** (`TOO_MANY_UNIONS`, a new `OpError` code), matching the `restorePerson` schema cap, so the inverse of a delete always fits one op (ledger T2 (3)).
- **`restorePerson` keeps later `order` and `status` edits** of a union that survived the delete with the other partner; only the person comes back (ledger T2 (4)).
- **`revertsSeq` and `revertsToSeq` are range-checked** inside the write transaction: `revertsSeq` must be at most the clan's version and `revertsToSeq` below it; otherwise `VALIDATION_ERROR` (400). Sending both is a 400.
- **The identity check runs before the clan-role check**, so another account's queue gets `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, never a 404 or 403 that would make the store drop it. A route opts in with `access: { clanRole, checksExpectedUser: true }`.
- **The history page loads its tree into the store** (summaries need names, reverts are checked locally). So the unsent-changes guard treats another tree's history page like its tree page and asks first.
- **An editor sees no "Revert" on a change whose inverse needs the Owner** (it sets the progenitor), because the server would refuse it.
- **The toolbar stays above the board on every screen size.** §7.5 asks for a mobile bottom bar with the rest in a "more" menu; with only ↶, ↷ and the save status there is no "more" menu yet, and a fixed bar would cover the tray and the board's buttons on small screens. Task 14 records the deviation; the bottom bar comes with the toolbar's other controls.
- **Board keys.** The board itself becomes a tab stop: arrow keys pan, `+`/`-` zoom, `0` fits the tree, with zoom buttons in its corner. Arrow keys on a focused card do nothing yet, because §7.8 keeps them for moving between relatives. A card that receives focus off screen is brought into view, and a newly added person is brought into view instead of refitting the whole tree (no refit on removals, ledger T10).
- **The `discarded` notice says when one dropped change was being sent** (`inFlight`), because that one may have been saved after all (ledger T8).
- **After `IDENTITY_MISMATCH` the queue is kept until the tab learns the account changed.** When the app then sees the other account (for example after signing in as it), the account-change rule drops the queue with the `discarded` notice, as §7.3 says. Signing in again with the account that made the changes sends them.
- **A reload on another tree's page** restores the queue of the tree it belongs to and starts sending it; the tree page that is open then loads its own tree, which drops the restored queue with the `discarded` notice (nothing can ask during a reload).

**Not in P6a** (ledger rows and roadmap items that belong to P6b–d or later)
- Ledger T1: `DateInfo` accepting an extra side that disagrees with `source`, empty text fields turned into `null`, deriving `CLEARABLE_PERSON_FIELDS` from the schema (P6b, profile panel and `DualDateInput`).
- Ledger T8: new child last relies on ObjectId time order (P6c, drag-reorder sets `siblingOrder`).
- Ledger T9: `keepNode` drops React Flow's `selected` (P6b, selection arrives with the profile panel).
- Ledger T12 FLAG(1): `TreeDialogs` keeps a stale dialog when the person vanishes (P6b replaces these dialogs with the panel and the card menu).
- Roadmap: "Add several people", drag to relate, cutting, the Loose photos tray (P6c); the profile panel, `DualDateInput`, date marks on cards, "Add parent" (P6b); Cloudinary photos (P6d).
- Spec §7.5 mobile bottom bar and "more" menu, and §7.8 arrow keys between relatives (with the toolbar's next controls and the card menu).

## Global Constraints

**Repository and language**
- Node `>=24.0.0`, `"packageManager": "pnpm@11.17.0"`, TypeScript `~6.0.3` (do not upgrade). Use plain `pnpm` (not `rtk pnpm`). Run every command from the repository root `D:\ReactJS\AncestorsTree`.
- Everything in the repository is in English (code, identifiers, comments, docs, commit messages). The UI is Vietnamese by default with English; user-facing text lives only in `apps/web/src/locales/{vi,en}.json`, with identical key sets (`locales.test.ts` enforces it). Avoid count plurals: phrase texts so one form fits every count (the existing `discarded` text does this).
- The spec is bilingual: any change to `2026-11-11-ancestors-tree-design.md` updates `2026-11-11-ancestors-tree-design.vi.md` in the same commit.
- User-facing names (spec §3): a Clan is a **family tree** ("cây gia phả"), the `owner` role is **Manager** ("Quản lý"). Code, API paths and the database keep `clan` and `owner`. In Vietnamese, undo is "Hoàn tác", redo "Làm lại", "Revert to this point" is "Quay về thời điểm này" (spec §6.4 VI).
- `packages/shared` and `apps/api` run natively on Node (ADR 0002): relative imports end in `.ts`. `apps/web` relative imports have no extension. `erasableSyntaxOnly` forbids enums, namespaces with values and constructor parameter properties everywhere.
- Web features import other features only through their `index.ts` (tests may import their own feature's files, and test data may import another feature's `fixtures.ts`). Shared UI comes only from `apps/web/src/shared/ui/index.ts`.
- Tree logic is pure and lives in `packages/shared/src/tree/` (imported as `@ancestors-tree/shared/tree`); API contracts live in `packages/shared/src/api/*.ts` and are exported from the root `@ancestors-tree/shared`.
- Follow the store's existing shapes: `ApplyResult = { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: ApplyFailure }`; notices are `TreeNotice` objects with a `kind` and the `clanId` they are about, shown app-wide by `TreeNoticeBanner` until dismissed.

**Secrets and privacy (never break these)**
- Never read, print or edit `apps/api/.env`.
- No credential-shaped strings in tests, fixtures or docs: no `user:password@host` strings, no realistic API keys or tokens. E-mail addresses use `example.test`; the only password is the existing test value in `apps/api/src/test/users.ts` and `e2e/support.ts`.

**API rules (from P3/P5)**
- Every route declares `access`; handlers return `{ status, body }` and never touch `res`; response bodies are parsed through their declared schemas.
- Error responses use only codes from `ERROR_CODES`. Run `pnpm docs:errors` after changing `packages/shared/src/errors.ts` or `OP_ERROR_CODES`; `pnpm check` runs `docs:errors:check`.
- Inside a transaction, run database calls one after another (no `Promise.all` on one session).

**Web rules (from P2/P3/P5)**
- Colors only from tokens; icons only from `lucide-react` with `strokeWidth={1.75}` and `aria-hidden="true"`; no emoji.
- Every control is keyboard-reachable with a visible focus outline and an accessible name that contains its visible text.
- Component tests use `const user = userEvent.setup()`, `mockApi` from `apps/web/src/test/api.ts` and `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` in `afterEach`.
- Read the Zustand store only through selectors that return a stable value (a primitive or an object already in the store). Never return a new object or array from a selector.

**Tests and commits**
- TDD in every task: write the failing test, run it and see it fail, write the code, run it and see it pass.
- Every task ends with its own tests passing and `pnpm check` passing (format, lint, typecheck, tests with coverage, error docs, build; about 2.5 minutes). Coverage thresholds: 70% lines overall, 90% for `packages/shared/src/**`.
- Before each commit, format new and changed files with `pnpm exec prettier --write <files>`; the code in this plan is not guaranteed to match Prettier's line breaks.
- One commit per task on branch `feat/p6a-undo-sync`, Conventional Commits, one PR for the whole plan. Write the message to `.superpowers/sdd/p6a-task-N-commit.txt` (git-ignored) and commit with `git commit -F <file>`. Every message ends with the trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Do not push.

### Task 1: Tree core hardening for undo

Undo makes `restorePerson` and `restoreUnion` everyday ops, so the gaps the P5 review flagged in them must close first: lookups that see inherited keys, a `restorePerson` snapshot that reuses the person's id or repeats a union, the inverse of a delete that cannot fit one op, a later status edit wiped by an undo, and a property test that never produced a restore op.

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/schemas.ts` (add `MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON` after `MAX_PERSONS`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/op-error.ts` (add `TOO_MANY_UNIONS`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/ops.ts` (restore cap, `sameOp`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.ts` (`getPerson`, `getUnion`, `addUnion`, `updateUnion`, `restorePerson`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/testing.ts` (`invariantViolations`, `isOwnAncestorSlow`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/tree/index.ts`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/locales/vi.json`, `apps/web/src/locales/en.json` (`tree.rules.TOO_MANY_UNIONS`)
- Modify: `docs/error-codes.md` (generated by `pnpm docs:errors`)
- Test: `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.test.ts`, `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.properties.test.ts`, `packages/shared/src/tree/ops.test.ts`, `packages/shared/src/tree/testing.test.ts` (new), `apps/web/src/locales/locales.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces:
  - `MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON = 100` (exported from `@ancestors-tree/shared/tree`).
  - `OpErrorCode` gains `'TOO_MANY_UNIONS'`.
  - `sameOp(a: Op, b: Op): boolean` — deep equality of two ops as JSON values; key order and `undefined` fields do not matter (exported from `@ancestors-tree/shared/tree`). Task 6 uses it to recognise a saved op whose answer was lost.
  - `restorePerson` now keeps the current `order` and `status` of a union that still exists.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

In `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.test.ts`, change the imports at the top to:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { applyOp, isOwnAncestor } from './apply-op.ts';
import { OpError, type OpErrorCode } from './op-error.ts';
import type { Op, SingleOp } from './ops.ts';
import { MAX_PERSONS, MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON, type Person, type Union } from './schemas.ts';
import type { TreeState } from './state.ts';
import { invariantViolations, makePerson, makeUnion, testId, treeOf } from './testing.ts';
```

Inside `describe('restorePerson', …)`, after the test `'checks the snapshot'`, add:

```ts
  it('refuses a snapshot that reuses the person id or repeats a union', () => {
    const restore = (unions: Union[]) => () =>
      applyOp(family(), { type: 'restorePerson', payload: { person: makePerson(D), unions } });
    expectOpError(restore([makeUnion(D, [D])]), 'DUPLICATE_ID');
    expectOpError(restore([makeUnion(V, [D]), makeUnion(V, [D])]), 'DUPLICATE_ID');
  });

  it('does not mistake inherited keys for people or unions', () => {
    const restore = (unions: Union[]) => () =>
      applyOp(family(), { type: 'restorePerson', payload: { person: makePerson(D), unions } });
    expectOpError(restore([makeUnion('constructor', [D])]), 'INVALID_ID');
    expectOpError(restore([makeUnion(V, [D, 'toString'])]), 'PERSON_NOT_FOUND');
  });

  it('keeps later changes to a union that kept the other partner', () => {
    const { tree: deleted, inverse } = applyOp(withInLaw(), {
      type: 'deletePerson',
      payload: { id: D },
    });
    const divorced = applyOp(deleted, {
      type: 'updateUnion',
      payload: { id: W, changes: { status: 'divorced', order: 2 } },
    }).tree;
    expect(applyOp(divorced, inverse).tree.unions[W]).toEqual({
      id: W,
      partnerIds: [C, D],
      order: 2,
      status: 'divorced',
    });
  });
```

After `describe('setProgenitor', …)`, add:

```ts
describe('inherited keys', () => {
  it('are never people or unions', () => {
    const tree = family();
    expectOpError(
      () => applyOp(tree, { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: 'constructor' } }),
      'PERSON_NOT_FOUND',
    );
    expectOpError(
      () =>
        applyOp(tree, {
          type: 'updateUnion',
          payload: { id: 'toString', changes: { status: 'divorced' } },
        }),
      'UNION_NOT_FOUND',
    );
    expectOpError(
      () =>
        applyOp(tree, { type: 'setParents', payload: { personId: C, parentUnionId: 'valueOf' } }),
      'UNION_NOT_FOUND',
    );
  });
});

describe('unions per person', () => {
  /** A has the maximum number of marriages, each with a different spouse; D is single. */
  function busy(): TreeState {
    const spouses = Array.from({ length: MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON }, (_, index) =>
      makePerson(testId(1000 + index), { gender: 'female' }),
    );
    const unions = spouses.map((spouse, index) => makeUnion(testId(2000 + index), [A, spouse.id]));
    return treeOf([makePerson(A), makePerson(D), ...spouses], unions);
  }

  it('stops a new marriage past the limit', () => {
    expectOpError(
      () => applyOp(busy(), { type: 'addUnion', payload: makeUnion(V, [D, A]) }),
      'TOO_MANY_UNIONS',
    );
    expectOpError(
      () => applyOp(busy(), { type: 'addUnion', payload: makeUnion(V, [A]) }),
      'TOO_MANY_UNIONS',
    );
    const single = applyOp(busy(), { type: 'addUnion', payload: makeUnion(V, [D]) });
    expect(single.tree.unions[V]).toEqual(makeUnion(V, [D]));
  });

  it('counts a partner moved into a union, but not the union being changed', () => {
    const tree = applyOp(busy(), { type: 'addUnion', payload: makeUnion(V, [D]) }).tree;
    expectOpError(
      () =>
        applyOp(tree, { type: 'updateUnion', payload: { id: V, changes: { partnerIds: [D, A] } } }),
      'TOO_MANY_UNIONS',
    );
    const first = testId(2000);
    const moved = applyOp(tree, {
      type: 'updateUnion',
      payload: { id: first, changes: { partnerIds: [A, D] } },
    });
    expect(moved.tree.unions[first]?.partnerIds).toEqual([A, D]);
  });

  it('refuses a restore that would pass the limit', () => {
    const restore = (tree: TreeState, unions: Union[]) => () =>
      applyOp(tree, { type: 'restorePerson', payload: { person: makePerson(K), unions } });
    expectOpError(restore(busy(), [makeUnion(W, [K, A])]), 'TOO_MANY_UNIONS');
    const tooMany = Array.from({ length: MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON + 1 }, (_, index) =>
      makeUnion(testId(3000 + index), [K]),
    );
    expectOpError(restore(family(), tooMany), 'TOO_MANY_UNIONS');
  });
});
```

In `describe('batch', …)`, replace the test `'fails as a whole and leaves the tree untouched'` with:

```ts
  it.each([0, 1, 2])('fails as a whole when op %i is refused', (position) => {
    const ops: SingleOp[] = [
      { type: 'addPerson', payload: makePerson(D) },
      { type: 'addPerson', payload: makePerson(E) },
    ];
    ops.splice(position, 0, { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: A } });
    const tree = family();
    const before = structuredClone(tree);
```

In `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.properties.test.ts`, the generator gains an `undo` command that applies the latest stored inverse, so restore ops are exercised, and every tree comparison becomes `toStrictEqual`. Replace the `Command` type and the `command` arbitrary with:

```ts
/** Indexes pick an existing person or union (modulo the count), so most commands apply. */
type Command =
  | { kind: 'addPerson'; parent: number | null }
  | { kind: 'addUnion'; a: number; b: number | null }
  | { kind: 'updatePerson'; target: number; name: string; clearBio: boolean }
  | { kind: 'updateUnion'; target: number; a: number; b: number | null }
  | { kind: 'deletePerson'; target: number }
  | { kind: 'deleteUnion'; target: number }
  | { kind: 'setParents'; target: number; union: number | null }
  | { kind: 'setProgenitor'; target: number | null }
  /** Applies the inverse of the latest change not undone yet (restore ops come from here). */
  | { kind: 'undo' };

const index = fc.nat({ max: 50 });
const command: fc.Arbitrary<Command> = fc.oneof(
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('addPerson' as const), parent: fc.option(index) }),
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('addUnion' as const), a: index, b: fc.option(index) }),
  fc.record({
    kind: fc.constant('updatePerson' as const),
    target: index,
    name: fc.string({ minLength: 1, maxLength: 8 }),
    clearBio: fc.boolean(),
  }),
  fc.record({
    kind: fc.constant('updateUnion' as const),
    target: index,
    a: index,
    b: fc.option(index),
  }),
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('deletePerson' as const), target: index }),
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('deleteUnion' as const), target: index }),
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('setParents' as const), target: index, union: fc.option(index) }),
  fc.record({ kind: fc.constant('setProgenitor' as const), target: fc.option(index) }),
  fc.constant({ kind: 'undo' as const }),
);
```

Replace `step`, keep `tryApply` and `idMaker`, and replace the whole `describe('applyOp properties', …)` block with:

```ts
/** Applies op; checks immutability, invariants and the inverse round trip on success. */
function step(tree: TreeState, op: Op): ApplyOpResult | null {
  const before = structuredClone(tree);
  const result = tryApply(tree, op);
  expect(tree).toStrictEqual(before);
  if (!result) return null;
  expect(invariantViolations(result.tree)).toEqual([]);
  expect(applyOp(result.tree, result.inverse).tree).toStrictEqual(before);
  return result;
}

/** How often the undo command applied each op type, over all runs. */
type Seen = Partial<Record<Op['type'], number>>;

/** Runs the commands from an empty tree; undo applies the latest inverse not used yet. */
function run(commands: readonly Command[], nextId: () => string, seen: Seen): TreeState {
  let tree = EMPTY_TREE;
  const undoStack: Op[] = [];
  for (const cmd of commands) {
    if (cmd.kind === 'undo') {
      const inverse = undoStack.pop();
      const result = inverse ? step(tree, inverse) : null;
      if (inverse && result) {
        tree = result.tree;
        seen[inverse.type] = (seen[inverse.type] ?? 0) + 1;
      }
      continue;
    }
    const op = toOp(tree, cmd, nextId);
    const result = op ? step(tree, op) : null;
    if (result) {
      tree = result.tree;
      undoStack.push(result.inverse);
    }
  }
  return tree;
}

describe('applyOp properties', () => {
  it('keeps the tree valid, never mutates it, and every inverse restores it exactly', () => {
    const seen: Seen = {};
    fc.assert(
      fc.property(fc.array(command, { maxLength: 60 }), (commands) => {
        run(commands, idMaker(), seen);
      }),
      { numRuns: 300 },
    );
    // The undo command really produced the restore ops this test is about.
    expect(seen.restorePerson ?? 0).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(seen.restoreUnion ?? 0).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });

  it('round-trips batches', () => {
    fc.assert(
      fc.property(
        fc.array(command, { maxLength: 30 }),
        fc.array(fc.array(command, { minLength: 1, maxLength: 4 }), { maxLength: 8 }),
        (setup, groups) => {
          const nextId = idMaker();
          let tree = run(setup, nextId, {});
          for (const group of groups) {
            // Build each child op against the tree as the batch will see it.
            const ops: SingleOp[] = [];
            let preview = tree;
            for (const cmd of group) {
              const op = toOp(preview, cmd, nextId);
              const result = op ? tryApply(preview, op) : null;
              if (op && result) {
                ops.push(op);
                preview = result.tree;
              }
            }
            const result = ops.length > 0 ? step(tree, { type: 'batch', payload: { ops } }) : null;
            if (result) tree = result.tree;
          }
        },
      ),
      { numRuns: 200 },
    );
  });
});
```

`idMaker` must now be declared before `describe` (it already is); move `step` above `run` if Prettier or lint complains about use before definition.

```ts
/** JSON text of a value with object keys sorted, so key order does not matter. */
function canonical(value: unknown): string {
  return JSON.stringify(value, (_key, inner: unknown) =>
    inner !== null && typeof inner === 'object' && !Array.isArray(inner)
      ? Object.fromEntries(
          Object.entries(inner).sort(([a], [b]) => (a < b ? -1 : a > b ? 1 : 0)),
        )
      : inner,
  );
}

/**
 * True when two ops are the same change as JSON values: what the server stored compared with
 * what this tab sent. Key order and undefined fields do not matter; array order does.
 */
export function sameOp(a: Op, b: Op): boolean {
  return canonical(a) === canonical(b);
}
```

In `packages/shared/src/tree/apply-op.ts`, add `MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON,` to the `./schemas.ts` import, then replace `getPerson` and `getUnion` with:

```ts
// Object.hasOwn: an id such as "constructor" must not find Object.prototype's members.
function getPerson(tree: TreeState, id: string): Person {
  if (!Object.hasOwn(tree.persons, id)) {
    throw new OpError('PERSON_NOT_FOUND', `Person ${id} does not exist`);
  }
  return tree.persons[id] as Person;
}

function getUnion(tree: TreeState, id: string): Union {
  if (!Object.hasOwn(tree.unions, id)) {
    throw new OpError('UNION_NOT_FOUND', `Union ${id} does not exist`);
  }
  return tree.unions[id] as Union;
}
```

After `assertPartners`, add:

```ts
function unionCount(tree: TreeState, personId: string, except?: string): number {
  let count = 0;
  for (const union of Object.values(tree.unions)) {
    if (union.id !== except && union.partnerIds.includes(personId)) count += 1;
  }
  return count;
}

/**
 * Keeps every person within MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON, so the restorePerson that undoes a delete
 * always fits one op. `except` is a union being changed: it is not counted twice.
 */
function assertRoomForUnions(
  tree: TreeState,
  partnerIds: readonly string[],
  except?: string,
): void {
  for (const partnerId of partnerIds) {
    if (unionCount(tree, partnerId, except) >= MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON) {
      throw new OpError('TOO_MANY_UNIONS', `Person ${partnerId} already has the most unions`);
    }
  }
}
```

In `addUnion`, after `assertPartners(tree, union.partnerIds);` add `assertRoomForUnions(tree, union.partnerIds);`.

In `updateUnion`, replace the `partnerIds` branch with:

```ts
  if (changes.partnerIds !== undefined) {
    assertPartners(tree, changes.partnerIds);
    assertRoomForUnions(tree, changes.partnerIds, id);
    previous.partnerIds = union.partnerIds;
    next.partnerIds = changes.partnerIds;
  }
```

Replace `restorePerson` with:

```ts
function restorePerson(tree: TreeState, person: Person, snapshots: readonly Union[]): SingleResult {
  assertNewId(tree, person.id);
  assertRoomForPerson(tree);
  if (snapshots.length > MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON) {
    throw new OpError('TOO_MANY_UNIONS', `Person ${person.id} would have too many unions`);
  }
  const snapshotIds = new Set(snapshots.map((snapshot) => snapshot.id));
  if (snapshotIds.size !== snapshots.length || snapshotIds.has(person.id)) {
    throw new OpError('DUPLICATE_ID', 'A union in the snapshot repeats an id');
  }
  const unions = { ...tree.unions };
  for (const snapshot of snapshots) {
    assertDistinctPartners(snapshot.partnerIds);
    if (!snapshot.partnerIds.includes(person.id)) {
      throw new OpError('INVALID_PARTNERS', `Union ${snapshot.id} does not include the person`);
    }
    for (const partnerId of snapshot.partnerIds) {
      if (partnerId !== person.id) getPerson(tree, partnerId);
    }
    if (Object.hasOwn(tree.unions, snapshot.id)) {
      const current = tree.unions[snapshot.id] as Union;
      // The union kept the other partner when the person was deleted; it may not have gained
      // anyone else since.
      if (!current.partnerIds.every((partnerId) => snapshot.partnerIds.includes(partnerId))) {
        throw new OpError('RESTORE_CONFLICT', `Union ${snapshot.id} has changed partners`);
      }
      // Only the person comes back: a later order or status edit of the union stays.
      unions[snapshot.id] = { ...current, partnerIds: snapshot.partnerIds };
    } else {
      assertNewId(tree, snapshot.id);
      unions[snapshot.id] = snapshot;
    }
  }
  const nextTree: TreeState = {
    ...tree,
    persons: withEntry(tree.persons, person.id, person),
    unions,
  };
  for (const partnerId of new Set(snapshots.flatMap((snapshot) => snapshot.partnerIds))) {
    if (unionCount(nextTree, partnerId) > MAX_UNIONS_PER_PERSON) {
      throw new OpError('TOO_MANY_UNIONS', `Person ${partnerId} would have too many unions`);
    }
  }
  if (person.parentUnionId !== undefined) getUnion(nextTree, person.parentUnionId);
  assertNoCycle(nextTree, [person.id]);
  return { tree: nextTree, inverse: { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: person.id } } };
}
```

In `packages/shared/src/tree/testing.ts`, in `isOwnAncestorSlow` replace the `union` line with

```ts

      unionId === undefined || !Object.hasOwn(tree.unions, unionId)
        ? undefined
        : tree.unions[unionId];
```

and replace `invariantViolations` with:

```ts
/** Every broken tree invariant as a short message; [] for a valid tree. */
export function invariantViolations(tree: TreeState): string[] {
  const problems: string[] = [];
  for (const [id, person] of Object.entries(tree.persons)) {
    if (person.id !== id) problems.push(`person ${id} is stored under another id`);
    if (person.parentUnionId !== undefined && !Object.hasOwn(tree.unions, person.parentUnionId)) {
      problems.push(`person ${id} points at a missing union`);
    }
    if (isOwnAncestorSlow(tree, id)) problems.push(`person ${id} is their own ancestor`);
  }
  for (const [id, union] of Object.entries(tree.unions)) {
    if (union.id !== id) problems.push(`union ${id} is stored under another id`);
    if (Object.hasOwn(tree.persons, id)) problems.push(`id ${id} is both a person and a union`);
    const partners = union.partnerIds;
    if (partners.length < 1 || partners.length > 2 || new Set(partners).size !== partners.length) {
      problems.push(`union ${id} has wrong partners`);
    }
    for (const partnerId of partners) {
      if (!Object.hasOwn(tree.persons, partnerId)) {
        problems.push(`union ${id} has a missing partner`);
      }
    }
  }
  if (tree.progenitorId !== null && !Object.hasOwn(tree.persons, tree.progenitorId)) {
    problems.push('the progenitor is missing');
  }
  return problems;
}
```

### Task 2: Carried over: the invite Membership transaction and the delete-tree tests

Accepting an invite while the Manager deletes the tree can leave a Membership for a tree that no longer exists. The accept now runs in a transaction that writes the Clan document, so it conflicts with the delete transaction (which deletes that document): one of them retries and then sees what the other did. The P5 review also asked for two sharper web tests (ledger T13).

**Files:**
- Modify: `apps/api/src/routes/invites.ts` (the accept handler)
- Test: `apps/api/src/routes/invites.test.ts`
- Test: `apps/web/src/features/clans/ClanSettingsPage.test.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces: nothing new; `POST /api/invites/:token/accept` keeps its contract and answers `INVALID_TOKEN` (400) when the tree is gone.

- [ ] **Step 3: Run the accept in a transaction**

In `apps/api/src/routes/invites.ts`, add `import mongoose from 'mongoose';` after the `zod` import, and replace the accept route's `handler` with:

```ts
    handler: async ({ params, userId }) => {
      const invite = await findUsableInvite(params.token);
      const clanId = invite.clanId.toString();
      try {
        const body = await mongoose.connection.transaction(async (session) => {
          const existing = await Membership.findOne({ clanId, userId: userId() }, null, {
            session,
          });
          if (existing) {
            // Never change an existing member's role through a link.
            return { clanId, role: ClanRoleSchema.parse(existing.role), alreadyMember: true };
          }
          // Removed by the owner after this link was made: only a newer link lets them back in.
          const removal = await ClanRemoval.findOne({ clanId, userId: userId() }, null, {
            session,
          });
          if (removal && removal.removedAt >= invite.createdAt) {
            throw new AppError(
              'FORBIDDEN',
              'You were removed from this clan. Ask the owner for a new link',
            );
          }
          // Writing the clan document makes this transaction conflict with a delete of the same
          // clan: one of them retries and then sees the other's result, so a membership is
          // never left behind for a deleted clan.
          const touched = await Clan.updateOne(
            { _id: clanId },
            { $currentDate: { updatedAt: true } },
            { session, timestamps: false },
          );
          if (touched.matchedCount === 0) {
            throw new AppError('INVALID_TOKEN', 'This invite link is wrong, expired or revoked');
          }
          await Membership.create([{ clanId, userId: userId(), role: invite.role }], { session });
          if (removal) await ClanRemoval.deleteOne({ _id: removal._id }, { session });
          return { clanId, role: InviteRoleSchema.parse(invite.role), alreadyMember: false };
        });
        return { status: 200, body };
      } catch (error) {
        // The same person accepted twice at the same moment: the unique index keeps one. A
        // duplicate key aborts the transaction, so the stored row is read outside it.
        if ((error as { code?: unknown }).code !== DUPLICATE_KEY) throw error;
        const stored = await Membership.findOne({ clanId, userId: userId() });
        if (!stored) throw error;
        return {
          status: 200,
          body: { clanId, role: ClanRoleSchema.parse(stored.role), alreadyMember: true },
        };
      }
    },
```

`timestamps: false` is needed: Mongoose would otherwise add its own `$set` of `updatedAt` next to `$currentDate` and MongoDB refuses two updates of one path. The existing test `'answers the duplicate-key race with the role actually stored'` mocks the first `Membership.findOne` with `mockResolvedValueOnce(null)`; passing the session as the third argument (not `.session(session)`) keeps that mock working.

### Task 3: The tree store knows the account

The queue must never go out under another account, and later tasks key the saved queue and the identity check by user. The store gets the account from the app shell, keeps it across a reset, and decides itself what an account change means. The `discarded` notice learns whether one dropped change was being sent at that moment (ledger T8).

- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.ts`
- Create: `apps/web/src/features/tree/useTreeAccount.ts`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/index.ts`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreeNoticeBanner.tsx`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/app/AppShell.tsx`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/locales/vi.json`, `apps/web/src/locales/en.json` (`tree.notice.discardedInFlight`)
- Test: `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/useTreeAccount.test.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreePage.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreeEditing.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreeBoard.test.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing new.
- Produces (all from `features/tree/store.ts`, re-exported by `features/tree/index.ts` where noted):

  - `TreeNotice` kind `discarded` is now `{ kind: 'discarded'; clanId: string; count: number; inFlight: boolean }`.
  - `resetTreeStore(): void` keeps `userId`; `signOutTreeStore(): void` (exported) also forgets the account.
  - `useTreeAccount(userId: string | undefined, updatedAt: number): void` (exported from `features/tree/useTreeAccount.ts` and the index).
  - `resumeAfterSignIn` and `discardTreeStateForAccountChange` are removed.
  - `apply()` answers `{ ok: false, reason: 'NOT_LOADED' }` while `userId` is `null`.

```ts
/** Stops the queue and forgets every answer still on its way. */
function stopSending(): void {
  session += 1;
  sending = false;
  needsReload = false;
  loading = undefined;
  clearRetry();
}

/**
 * Forgets the tree and the queue, including the module-level send state: answers still on their
 * way are ignored and nothing more is sent. Keeps the account. For the user discarding unsent
 * changes, a deleted tree, and tests.
 */
export function resetTreeStore(): void {
  const { userId } = useTreeStore.getState();
  stopSending();
  useTreeStore.setState({ ...INITIAL, userId });
}

/** Sign-out: forgets everything, the account too. */
export function signOutTreeStore(): void {
  stopSending();
  useTreeStore.setState(INITIAL);
}

/** Another account signed in: what the previous one left unsent is dropped, with a notice. */
function discardForAccountChange(): void {
  const { pending, clanId } = useTreeStore.getState();
  const inFlight = sending && !needsReload;
  signOutTreeStore();
  if (pending.length > 0 && clanId !== null) {
    useTreeStore.setState({
      notice: { kind: 'discarded', clanId, count: pending.length, inFlight },
    });
  }
}
```

Create `apps/web/src/features/tree/useTreeAccount.ts`:

```ts
import { useLayoutEffect } from 'react';
import { useTreeStore } from './store';

/**
 * Tells the tree store which account is signed in. `updatedAt` is the current-user query's
 * `dataUpdatedAt`: it changes whenever a sign-in sets the user again, also with the same account,
 * so a queue stopped by a lost session (or by another account's cookie) resumes then.
 *
 * A layout effect runs before every passive effect of the same commit, so the queue of another
 * account is gone before a page (such as the tree page loading its tree) can send it.
 */
export function useTreeAccount(userId: string | undefined, updatedAt: number): void {
  useLayoutEffect(() => {
    if (userId !== undefined) useTreeStore.getState().setAccount(userId);
  }, [userId, updatedAt]);
}
```


### Task 4: Identity check and revert fields on `POST /ops`

A tab can hold account A's unsent queue after account B signed in in another tab; the shared cookie would save A's changes under B's name. Every `POST /ops` now carries `expectedUserId`, and the server answers `409 IDENTITY_MISMATCH` before the clan-role check (a 404 or 403 would make the client drop the queue). The same request learns the two fields undo and history need: `revertsSeq` and `revertsToSeq`. This task also adds the `/ops` tests the P5 review deferred.

**Files:**
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/api/tree.ts` (`SubmitOpRequestSchema`)
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/errors.ts`, `packages/shared/src/errors.test.ts`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/clans/access.ts` (`createRequireExpectedUser`)
- Modify: `apps/api/src/http/routeRegistry.ts` (`Access`, `AccessPolicies`, `accessMiddleware`, `describeAccess`)
- Modify: `apps/api/src/app.ts` (the `clanRole` policy)
- Modify: `apps/api/src/models/Operation.ts`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/routes/tree.ts` (POST handler)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.ts`, `SaveStatus.tsx`, `TreeNoticeBanner.tsx`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/locales/vi.json`, `apps/web/src/locales/en.json`
- Modify: `docs/error-codes.md` (generated)
- Test: `apps/api/src/routes/tree.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/clans.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreeEditing.test.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TreeStoreState.userId` (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - `SubmitOpRequest = { op: Op; baseVersion: number; expectedUserId: string; revertsSeq?: number; revertsToSeq?: number }` (both revert fields at least 1, never both).
  - `ErrorCode` gains `'IDENTITY_MISMATCH'` (409).
  - `Access = 'public' | 'signedIn' | { clanRole: ClanRole; checksExpectedUser?: true }`; `AccessPolicies.clanRole(role: ClanRole, options: { expectedUser: boolean }): RequestHandler[]`.
  - Operation documents may hold `revertsSeq` and `revertsToSeq` (numbers); Task 5 returns them.
  - Store: `TreeStoreState.otherAccount: boolean`; `TreeNotice` kind `{ kind: 'otherAccount'; clanId: string }`.

```ts
describe('the expected user of POST /api/clans/:id/ops', () => {
  it('answers 409 IDENTITY_MISMATCH before the role check and changes nothing', async () => {
    const { editor, viewer, clanId } = await setup();
    const stranger = await signUp(app);
    for (const member of [editor, viewer, stranger]) {
      const response = await member.agent
        .post(`/api/clans/${clanId}/ops`)
        .send({ op: addPerson(person()), baseVersion: 0, expectedUserId: newObjectId() });
      expect(response.status).toBe(409);
      expect(response.body.error.code).toBe('IDENTITY_MISMATCH');
    }
    expect(await PersonModel.countDocuments({ clanId })).toBe(0);
  });

  it('is required', async () => {
    const { editor, clanId } = await setup();
    const response = await editor.agent
      .post(`/api/clans/${clanId}/ops`)
      .send({ op: addPerson(person()), baseVersion: 0 });
    expect(response.status).toBe(400);
    expect(response.body.error.code).toBe('VALIDATION_ERROR');

describe('reverts on POST /api/clans/:id/ops', () => {
  it('stores revertsSeq with the op', async () => {
    const { editor, clanId } = await setup();
    const tu = person();
    await submit(editor, clanId, addPerson(tu), 0);
    const undo: Op = { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: tu.id } };
    expect((await submit(editor, clanId, undo, 1, { revertsSeq: 1 })).status).toBe(200);
    const stored = await OperationModel.findOne({ clanId, seq: 2 }).lean();
    expect(stored?.revertsSeq).toBe(1);
    expect(stored?.revertsToSeq).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('checks the range of revertsSeq and revertsToSeq', async () => {
    const { owner, clanId } = await setup();
    await submit(owner, clanId, addPerson(person()), 0);
    const op = addPerson(person());
    for (const extra of [
      { revertsSeq: 2 },
      { revertsSeq: 0 },
      { revertsToSeq: 1 },
      { revertsSeq: 1, revertsToSeq: 1 },
    ]) {
      const response = await submit(owner, clanId, op, 1, extra);
      expect(response.status, JSON.stringify(extra)).toBe(400);
      expect(response.body.error.code).toBe('VALIDATION_ERROR');
    }
    expect((await Clan.findById(clanId))?.version).toBe(1);
  });

  it('lets only the Manager revert the tree to a point', async () => {
    const { owner, editor, clanId } = await setup();
    const tu = person();
    const hoa = person();
    await submit(owner, clanId, addPerson(tu), 0);
    await submit(owner, clanId, addPerson(hoa), 1);
    const back: Op = { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: hoa.id } };
    const denied = await submit(editor, clanId, back, 2, { revertsToSeq: 1 });
    expect(denied.status).toBe(403);
    expect((await submit(owner, clanId, back, 2, { revertsToSeq: 1 })).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await OperationModel.findOne({ clanId, seq: 3 }).lean())?.revertsToSeq).toBe(1);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Check the expected user before the clan role**

In `apps/api/src/clans/access.ts`, add at the end:

```ts
/**
 * For routes that act for the account the client expects (spec §6.1): a tab may still hold
 * another account's unsent changes after a sign-in in another tab, under the shared cookie.
 * Runs after requireAuth and before the clan role, so the mismatch is never hidden behind a
 * 404 or 403 that would make the client drop those changes. A missing or malformed value is
 * left to body validation (400).
 */
export function createRequireExpectedUser(): RequestHandler {
  return (req, res, next) => {
    const locals = getLocals(res);
    if (!locals.userId) throw new Error('requireExpectedUser must run after requireAuth');
    const body: unknown = req.body;
    const expected =
      typeof body === 'object' && body !== null
        ? (body as { expectedUserId?: unknown }).expectedUserId
        : undefined;
    if (IdSchema.safeParse(expected).success && expected !== locals.userId) {
      throw new AppError('IDENTITY_MISMATCH', 'These changes were made with another account');
    }
    next();
  };
}
```

In `apps/api/src/http/routeRegistry.ts`, replace `Access` and `AccessPolicies` with:

```ts
/**
 * Who may call a route. Every route must say it explicitly. `checksExpectedUser`: the body's
 * `expectedUserId` must be the signed-in user, checked before the role (IDENTITY_MISMATCH).
 */
export type Access =
  | 'public'
  | 'signedIn'
  | { clanRole: ClanRole; checksExpectedUser?: true };

/** The middleware that enforces each access level (built by createApp). */
export interface AccessPolicies {
  signedIn: RequestHandler[];
  /** Must also authenticate: it runs instead of `signedIn`, not after it. */
  clanRole: (role: ClanRole, options: { expectedUser: boolean }) => RequestHandler[];
}
```

and replace `accessMiddleware` and `describeAccess` with:

```ts
function accessMiddleware(access: Access, policies: AccessPolicies): RequestHandler[] {
  if (access === 'public') return [];
  if (access === 'signedIn') return policies.signedIn;
  return policies.clanRole(access.clanRole, { expectedUser: access.checksExpectedUser === true });
}

function describeAccess(access: Access): string | undefined {
  if (access === 'public') return undefined;
  if (access === 'signedIn') return 'Requires a signed-in user.';
  const role = `Requires the ${access.clanRole} role (or higher) in the clan.`;
  return access.checksExpectedUser
    ? `${role} The body's expectedUserId must be the signed-in user (409 IDENTITY_MISMATCH).`
    : role;
}
```

```ts
      createRequireClanRole(role),
```

```ts
    handler: async ({ params, body, membership, userId }) => {
      const { op, baseVersion, revertsSeq, revertsToSeq } = body;
      const isOwner = membership().role === 'owner';
      if (needsOwner(op) && !isOwner) {
        throw new AppError('FORBIDDEN', 'Only the Manager can choose the progenitor');
      }
      if (revertsToSeq !== undefined && !isOwner) {
        throw new AppError('FORBIDDEN', 'Only the Manager can revert the family tree to a point');
      }
      const seq = await mongoose.connection.transaction(async (session) => {
        const clan = await findClan(params.id, session);
        if (clan.version !== baseVersion) {
          throw new AppError(
            'VERSION_CONFLICT',
            `The family tree is at version ${clan.version}, not ${baseVersion}`,
          );
        }
        // A revert names a change that exists: at most the current version, and a point
        // strictly before it.
        if (revertsSeq !== undefined && revertsSeq > clan.version) {
          throw new AppError('VALIDATION_ERROR', `There is no change ${revertsSeq} to revert`);
        }
        if (revertsToSeq !== undefined && revertsToSeq >= clan.version) {
          throw new AppError('VALIDATION_ERROR', `Nothing was changed after ${revertsToSeq}`);
        }
```

```ts
  /** The user is back (apply, load, or a sign-in after a stop): send again. */
  function resume(): void {
```

### Task 5: History API

The history page needs what `GET /ops` does not give: newest first, pages that go backwards, the author's display name and the stored inverse. A separate endpoint keeps `/ops` lean for the rebase (decision recorded at the top).

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/shared/src/api/history.ts`
- Modify: `packages/shared/src/index.ts`
- Create: `apps/api/src/users/names.ts`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/routes/invites.ts` (use the shared `namesOf`)
- Create: `apps/api/src/routes/history.ts`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/app.ts`
- Test: `apps/api/src/routes/history.test.ts` (new), `packages/shared/src/api/history.test.ts` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the Operation fields `revertsSeq` and `revertsToSeq` (Task 4).
- Produces (from `@ancestors-tree/shared`):
  - `HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE = 50`, `HISTORY_PAGE_MAX = 100`.
  - `HistoryQuerySchema` (`{ before?: number; limit: number }`, query strings coerced).
  - `HistoryEntry = { seq: number; userId: string; authorName: string; createdAt: string; op: Op; inverse: Op; revertsSeq?: number; revertsToSeq?: number }` and `HistoryEntrySchema`.
  - `HistoryPage = { entries: HistoryEntry[]; nextBefore: number | null }` and `HistoryPageSchema`.
  - `GET /api/clans/:id/history?before=&limit=` (viewer and up): entries with `seq < before`, newest first, at most `limit`; `nextBefore` is the seq to pass as `before` for the next page, `null` on the last page.
  - API: `namesOf(userIds: readonly string[]): Promise<Map<string, string>>` in `apps/api/src/users/names.ts`.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { HISTORY_PAGE_MAX, HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE, HistoryQuerySchema } from './history.ts';

describe('HistoryQuerySchema', () => {
  it('reads query strings and defaults the page size', () => {
    expect(HistoryQuerySchema.parse({})).toEqual({ limit: HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE });
    expect(HistoryQuerySchema.parse({ before: '12', limit: '5' })).toEqual({ before: 12, limit: 5 });
  });

  it('refuses a page that is too big and a seq below 1', () => {
    expect(HistoryQuerySchema.safeParse({ limit: String(HISTORY_PAGE_MAX + 1) }).success).toBe(false);
    expect(HistoryQuerySchema.safeParse({ before: '0' }).success).toBe(false);
```

Create `apps/api/src/routes/history.test.ts`:

```ts
import { HISTORY_PAGE_MAX } from '@ancestors-tree/shared';
import { newObjectId, type Op, type Person } from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import request from 'supertest';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { User } from '../models/User.ts';
import { createTestApp } from '../test/app.ts';
import { addMember, createClan } from '../test/clans.ts';
import { useTestDatabase } from '../test/database.ts';
import { signUp } from '../test/users.ts';

useTestDatabase();

const { app } = createTestApp();

type Member = Awaited<ReturnType<typeof signUp>>;
type Entry = { seq: number };

function person(fullName: string): Person {
  return {
    id: newObjectId(),
    fullName,
    aliases: [],
    gender: 'male',
    death: { isDeceased: false },
    isAdopted: false,
  };
}

async function submit(
  member: Member,
  clanId: string,
  op: Op,
  baseVersion: number,
  extra: Record<string, unknown> = {},
): Promise<void> {
  const response = await member.agent
    .post(`/api/clans/${clanId}/ops`)
    .send({ op, baseVersion, expectedUserId: member.user.id, ...extra });
  expect(response.status).toBe(200);
}

async function setup() {
  const owner = await signUp(app, { name: 'Ông Thịnh' });
  const editor = await signUp(app, { name: 'Chị Lan' });
  const viewer = await signUp(app);
  const clan = await createClan(owner.agent);
  await addMember(clan.id, editor.user.id, 'editor');
  await addMember(clan.id, viewer.user.id, 'viewer');
  return { owner, editor, viewer, clanId: clan.id, path: `/api/clans/${clan.id}/history` };
}

describe('GET /api/clans/:id/history', () => {
  it('lists changes newest first with author names, inverses and revert links', async () => {
    const { owner, editor, viewer, clanId, path } = await setup();
    const tu = person('Nguyễn Văn Tư');
    await submit(owner, clanId, { type: 'addPerson', payload: tu }, 0);
    const bio: Op = { type: 'updatePerson', payload: { id: tu.id, changes: { bio: 'Cụ tổ' } } };
    await submit(editor, clanId, bio, 1);
    const undo: Op = { type: 'updatePerson', payload: { id: tu.id, changes: { bio: null } } };
    await submit(editor, clanId, undo, 2, { revertsSeq: 2 });

    const response = await viewer.agent.get(path);
    expect(response.status).toBe(200);
    expect(response.body.nextBefore).toBeNull();
    expect(response.body.entries.map((entry: Entry) => entry.seq)).toEqual([3, 2, 1]);
    expect(response.body.entries[0]).toMatchObject({
      seq: 3,
      userId: editor.user.id,
      authorName: 'Chị Lan',
      op: undo,
      inverse: bio,
      revertsSeq: 2,
    });
    expect(response.body.entries[2]).toMatchObject({
      authorName: 'Ông Thịnh',
      op: { type: 'addPerson', payload: tu },
      inverse: { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: tu.id } },
    });
    expect(response.body.entries[2]).not.toHaveProperty('revertsSeq');
    expect(Date.parse(response.body.entries[0].createdAt)).not.toBeNaN();
  });

  it('pages backwards with `before` and `limit`', async () => {
    const { owner, viewer, clanId, path } = await setup();
    for (let version = 0; version < 5; version += 1) {
      await submit(owner, clanId, { type: 'addPerson', payload: person(`Người ${version}`) }, version);
    }
    const seqs = (body: { entries: Entry[] }) => body.entries.map((entry) => entry.seq);
    const first = (await viewer.agent.get(`${path}?limit=2`)).body;
    expect(seqs(first)).toEqual([5, 4]);
    expect(first.nextBefore).toBe(4);
    const second = (await viewer.agent.get(`${path}?limit=2&before=4`)).body;
    expect(seqs(second)).toEqual([3, 2]);
    expect(second.nextBefore).toBe(2);
    const last = (await viewer.agent.get(`${path}?limit=2&before=2`)).body;
    expect(seqs(last)).toEqual([1]);
    expect(last.nextBefore).toBeNull();
  });

  it('keeps the id but no name for an account that no longer exists', async () => {
    const { editor, viewer, clanId, path } = await setup();
    await submit(editor, clanId, { type: 'addPerson', payload: person('Trần Văn Bình') }, 0);
    await User.deleteOne({ _id: editor.user.id });
    const [entry] = (await viewer.agent.get(path)).body.entries;
    expect(entry).toMatchObject({ userId: editor.user.id, authorName: '' });
  });

  it('is for members only and validates the query', async () => {
    const { viewer, path } = await setup();
    const stranger = await signUp(app);
    expect((await stranger.agent.get(path)).status).toBe(404);
    expect((await request(app).get(path)).status).toBe(401);
    expect((await viewer.agent.get(`${path}?limit=${HISTORY_PAGE_MAX + 1}`)).status).toBe(400);
    expect((await viewer.agent.get(`${path}?before=0`)).status).toBe(400);
  });
});
```

Create `packages/shared/src/api/history.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { OpSchema } from '../tree/ops.ts';
import { IdSchema } from './common.ts';

/** Entries per history page unless the client asks for fewer, and the most it may ask for. */
export const HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE = 50;
export const HISTORY_PAGE_MAX = 100;

/** GET /api/clans/:id/history. */
export const HistoryQuerySchema = z.object({
  /** Only changes with a smaller seq: the next, older page. Omitted for the newest. */
  before: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).optional(),
  limit: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(HISTORY_PAGE_MAX).default(HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE),
});

/** One saved change (spec §6.4). */
export const HistoryEntrySchema = z.object({
  seq: z.number().int().min(1),
  userId: IdSchema,
  /** The author's display name today; '' when the account no longer exists. */
  authorName: z.string(),
  createdAt: z.iso.datetime(),
  op: OpSchema,
  /** The op that undoes this change, computed when it was saved ("Revert" sends it). */
  inverse: OpSchema,
  /** This change undid or reverted the change with this seq. */
  revertsSeq: z.number().int().min(1).optional(),
  /** This change reverted the tree to how it was after this seq. */
  revertsToSeq: z.number().int().min(1).optional(),
});

export const HistoryPageSchema = z.object({
  entries: z.array(HistoryEntrySchema),
  /** Pass as `before` for the next page; null when this page reaches the first change. */
  nextBefore: z.number().int().min(1).nullable(),
});

export type HistoryQuery = z.infer<typeof HistoryQuerySchema>;
export type HistoryEntry = z.infer<typeof HistoryEntrySchema>;
export type HistoryPage = z.infer<typeof HistoryPageSchema>;
```

In `packages/shared/src/index.ts`, add at the end:

```ts
  HISTORY_PAGE_MAX,
  HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE,
  HistoryEntrySchema,
  HistoryPageSchema,
  HistoryQuerySchema,
  type HistoryEntry,
  type HistoryPage,
  type HistoryQuery,
} from './api/history.ts';
```

```ts
import { User } from '../models/User.ts';

/** Display names by user id; the ids of deleted accounts are missing from the map. */
export async function namesOf(userIds: readonly string[]): Promise<Map<string, string>> {
  const users = await User.find({ _id: { $in: [...new Set(userIds)] } });
  return new Map(users.map((user) => [user.id as string, user.name]));
}
```

In `apps/api/src/routes/invites.ts`, delete the local `namesOf` function and the `User` import, and add `import { namesOf } from '../users/names.ts';`.

Create `apps/api/src/routes/history.ts`:

```ts
import { HistoryPageSchema, HistoryQuerySchema, IdSchema, type HistoryEntry } from '@ancestors-tree/shared';
import type { Op } from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import type { Types } from 'mongoose';
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { RouteRegistry } from '../http/routeRegistry.ts';
import { OperationModel } from '../models/Operation.ts';
import { namesOf } from '../users/names.ts';

const ClanParamsSchema = z.object({ id: IdSchema });

interface HistoryRecord {
  seq: number;
  userId: Types.ObjectId;
  type: Op['type'];
  payload: unknown;
  inverse: unknown;
  createdAt: Date;
  revertsSeq?: number;
  revertsToSeq?: number;
}

function toHistoryEntry(record: HistoryRecord, names: Map<string, string>): HistoryEntry {
  const userId = record.userId.toString();
  return {
    seq: record.seq,
    userId,
    authorName: names.get(userId) ?? '',
    createdAt: record.createdAt.toISOString(),
    // Stored from a parsed op and applyOp's inverse, so both are valid ops.
    op: { type: record.type, payload: record.payload } as Op,
    inverse: record.inverse as Op,
    ...(record.revertsSeq === undefined ? {} : { revertsSeq: record.revertsSeq }),
    ...(record.revertsToSeq === undefined ? {} : { revertsToSeq: record.revertsToSeq }),
  };
}

export function registerHistoryRoutes(api: RouteRegistry): void {
  api.define({
    method: 'get',
    path: '/clans/:id/history',
    summary: 'Saved changes, newest first, with their authors and inverses',
    tags: ['Tree'],
    access: { clanRole: 'viewer' },
    request: { params: ClanParamsSchema, query: HistoryQuerySchema },
    responses: { 200: { description: 'One page of the history', schema: HistoryPageSchema } },
    handler: async ({ params, query }) => {
      const records = await OperationModel.find({
        clanId: params.id,
        ...(query.before === undefined ? {} : { seq: { $lt: query.before } }),
      })
        .sort({ seq: -1 })
        // One more than asked: it tells whether an older page exists.
        .limit(query.limit + 1)
        .lean<HistoryRecord[]>();
      const page = records.slice(0, query.limit);
      const names = await namesOf(page.map((record) => record.userId.toString()));
      const oldest = page.at(-1);
      return {
        status: 200,
        body: {
          entries: page.map((record) => toHistoryEntry(record, names)),
          nextBefore: records.length > query.limit && oldest ? oldest.seq : null,
        },
      };
    },
  });
}
```

In `apps/api/src/app.ts`, add `import { registerHistoryRoutes } from './routes/history.ts';` after the tree routes import and call `registerHistoryRoutes(api);` right after `registerTreeRoutes(api);`.

### Task 6: Queue entries and the 409 rebase

On `409 VERSION_CONFLICT` the store stops dropping the queue: it reads the missed operations, applies them to its confirmed tree, and replays its pending entries on top (spec §6.3). An entry that no longer applies is dropped with the `rejected` notice and the reason. The queue becomes a list of entries with local ids, which Task 8 needs to link an undo to the change it undoes. The third conflict in a row, a gap in the log, or a missed op that does not apply still reloads the whole tree.

- Create: `apps/web/src/features/tree/pending.ts`
- Create: `apps/web/src/features/tree/rebase.ts`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/api.ts` (`fetchOpsSince`)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.ts` (rewritten below)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/format.ts` (`rejectionMessage`)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/tree/fixtures.ts` (`GET /ops` route, `OTHER_USER_ID`, `remoteEntry`)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/locales/vi.json`, `apps/web/src/locales/en.json` (`tree.notice.rejectedDependents`)
- Test: `apps/web/src/features/tree/pending.test.ts` (new), `apps/web/src/features/tree/rebase.test.ts` (new), `apps/web/src/features/tree/api.test.ts` (new), `apps/web/src/features/tree/format.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/store.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/tree/TreeEditing.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/features/auth/LoginPage.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/features/auth/RegisterPage.test.tsx`

Create `apps/web/src/features/tree/pending.test.ts`:

```ts
import {
  OpError,
  treeStateFromLists,
  type Op,
  type TreeState,
} from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { ApiError } from '../../shared/lib/apiError';
import { FAMILY, fixturePerson, LOOSE, SON } from './fixtures';
import { replayPending, resolveRefs, revertFieldsOf, type PendingEntry } from './pending';

const NEW_ID = '64b7f0c2a1b2c3d4e5f6a0f1';
const family = (): TreeState =>
  treeStateFromLists(FAMILY.progenitorId, FAMILY.persons, FAMILY.unions);
const bio = (text: string): Op => ({ type: 'updatePerson', payload: { id: SON, changes: { bio: text } } });
const add: Op = { type: 'addPerson', payload: fixturePerson(NEW_ID, 'Nguyễn Văn Út') };
const removeNew: Op = { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: NEW_ID } };

describe('replayPending', () => {
  it('applies the entries that fit and reports why the others were dropped', () => {
    const entries: PendingEntry[] = [
      { id: 1, op: bio('Con trưởng') },
      { id: 2, op: removeNew },
      { id: 3, op: { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: LOOSE } } },
    ];
    const result = replayPending(family(), entries);
    expect(result.kept.map((entry) => entry.id)).toEqual([1, 3]);
    expect(result.dropped).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(result.dropped[0]?.entry.id).toBe(2);
    expect(result.dropped[0]?.error).toBeInstanceOf(OpError);
    expect((result.dropped[0]?.error as OpError).code).toBe('PERSON_NOT_FOUND');
    expect(result.tree.persons[SON]?.bio).toBe('Con trưởng');
    expect(result.tree.persons[LOOSE]).toBeUndefined();
    // The inverse of each kept entry, computed on the replayed tree.
    expect(result.inverses.get(1)).toEqual({
      type: 'updatePerson',
      payload: { id: SON, changes: { bio: null } },
    });
    expect(result.inverses.get(3)?.type).toBe('restorePerson');
  });

  it('drops the undo of a change that was dropped, even when it would still apply', () => {
    const entries: PendingEntry[] = [
      { id: 7, op: bio('Lần hai'), reverts: { pendingId: 5 } },
      { id: 8, op: bio('Lần ba') },
    ];
    const result = replayPending(family(), entries, { alreadyDropped: [5] });
    expect(result.dropped).toEqual([{ entry: entries[0], error: null }]);
    expect(result.kept).toEqual([entries[1]]);
  });

  it('drops a revert to a point when someone else saved a change', () => {
    const entry: PendingEntry = { id: 1, op: bio('x'), revertsToSeq: 2 };
    expect(replayPending(family(), [entry]).kept).toEqual([entry]);
    const outdated = replayPending(family(), [entry], { othersSaved: true });
    expect(outdated.kept).toEqual([]);
    expect(outdated.dropped[0]?.error).toBeInstanceOf(ApiError);
    expect(outdated.dropped[0]?.error).toMatchObject({ code: 'VERSION_CONFLICT' });
  });
});

describe('resolveRefs and revertFieldsOf', () => {
  it('names a saved change by its seq', () => {
    const entries: PendingEntry[] = [
      { id: 2, op: removeNew, reverts: { pendingId: 1 } },
      { id: 3, op: add, reverts: { pendingId: 9 } },
    ];
    const resolved = resolveRefs(entries, 1, 12);
    expect(resolved[0]?.reverts).toEqual({ seq: 12 });
    expect(resolved[1]).toBe(entries[1]);
    expect(revertFieldsOf(resolved[0] as PendingEntry)).toEqual({ revertsSeq: 12 });
    // Not saved yet: nothing to name.
    expect(revertFieldsOf(resolved[1] as PendingEntry)).toEqual({});
    expect(revertFieldsOf({ id: 4, op: add, revertsToSeq: 3 })).toEqual({ revertsToSeq: 3 });
  });
});
```

Create `apps/web/src/features/tree/rebase.test.ts`:

```ts
import { OpError, treeStateFromLists, type Op, type TreeState } from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { FAMILY, fixturePerson, LOOSE, ME, OTHER_USER_ID, remoteEntry, SON } from './fixtures';
import type { PendingEntry } from './pending';
import { rebase } from './rebase';

const NEW_ID = '64b7f0c2a1b2c3d4e5f6a0f1';
const CHILD = '64b7f0c2a1b2c3d4e5f6a0f2';
const LOOSE_UNION = '64b7f0c2a1b2c3d4e5f6b0f1';
const family = (): TreeState =>
  treeStateFromLists(FAMILY.progenitorId, FAMILY.persons, FAMILY.unions);
const bio = (text: string): Op => ({ type: 'updatePerson', payload: { id: SON, changes: { bio: text } } });
/** Someone gave Bình (LOOSE) a child. */
const looseChild: Op = {
  type: 'batch',
  payload: {
    ops: [
      { type: 'addUnion', payload: { id: LOOSE_UNION, partnerIds: [LOOSE], order: 1, status: 'married' } },
      { type: 'addPerson', payload: fixturePerson(CHILD, 'Trần Văn Cường', { parentUnionId: LOOSE_UNION }) },
    ],
  },
};
const input = (pending: PendingEntry[], remote = [remoteEntry(4, looseChild)]) => ({
  confirmedTree: family(),
  confirmedVersion: 3,
  pending,
  remote,
  userId: ME.id,
});

describe('rebase', () => {
  it('applies the missed ops, then replays the queue on top', () => {
    const result = rebase(input([{ id: 1, op: bio('Con trưởng') }]));
    if (!result.ok) throw new Error('expected a rebase');
    expect(result.confirmedVersion).toBe(4);
    expect(result.confirmedTree.persons[CHILD]).toBeDefined();
    expect(result.confirmedTree.persons[SON]?.bio).toBeUndefined();
    expect(result.tree.persons[CHILD]).toBeDefined();
    expect(result.tree.persons[SON]?.bio).toBe('Con trưởng');
    expect(result.pending.map((entry) => entry.id)).toEqual([1]);
    expect(result.saved).toBeNull();
  });

  it('drops a pending change the missed ops made impossible', () => {
    const result = rebase(input([{ id: 1, op: { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: LOOSE } } }]));
    if (!result.ok) throw new Error('expected a rebase');
    expect(result.pending).toEqual([]);
    expect((result.dropped[0]?.error as OpError).code).toBe('PERSON_HAS_CHILDREN');
    expect(result.tree.persons[LOOSE]).toBeDefined();
  });

  it('recognises the first queued op, saved before its answer was lost', () => {
    const pending: PendingEntry[] = [
      { id: 1, op: bio('Con trưởng') },
      { id: 2, op: bio('Con cả'), reverts: { pendingId: 1 } },
    ];
    const result = rebase(input(pending, [remoteEntry(4, bio('Con trưởng'), ME.id)]));
    if (!result.ok) throw new Error('expected a rebase');
    expect(result.saved).toEqual({ entry: pending[0], seq: 4 });
    expect(result.pending).toEqual([{ id: 2, op: bio('Con cả'), reverts: { seq: 4 } }]);
    expect(result.tree.persons[SON]?.bio).toBe('Con cả');
  });

  it('does not take the same op saved by someone else for ours', () => {
    const result = rebase(input([{ id: 1, op: bio('x') }], [remoteEntry(4, bio('x'), OTHER_USER_ID)]));
    if (!result.ok) throw new Error('expected a rebase');
    expect(result.saved).toBeNull();
    expect(result.pending).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it('asks for a reload when the log has a gap or does not apply', () => {
    expect(rebase(input([], [remoteEntry(5, looseChild)])).ok).toBe(false);
    const impossible: Op = { type: 'deletePerson', payload: { id: NEW_ID } };
    expect(rebase(input([], [remoteEntry(4, impossible)])).ok).toBe(false);
  });

  it('drops a revert to a point when anyone else saved a change', () => {
    const result = rebase(input([{ id: 1, op: bio('x'), revertsToSeq: 2 }]));
    if (!result.ok) throw new Error('expected a rebase');
    expect(result.pending).toEqual([]);
    expect(result.dropped[0]?.error).toMatchObject({ code: 'VERSION_CONFLICT' });
  });
});
```

Create `apps/web/src/features/tree/pending.ts`:

```ts
import { applyOp, OpError, type ApplyOpResult, type Op, type TreeState } from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import { ApiError } from '../../shared/lib/apiError';

/** A change named by its seq once saved, or by its local id while it waits to be sent. */
export type OpRef = { seq: number } | { pendingId: number };

/** One change the server has not accepted yet. */
export interface PendingEntry {
  /** Local id, unique in this tab: links the entry to its undo and redo steps. */
  id: number;
  op: Op;
  /** The change this entry undoes or reverts; sent as revertsSeq once that change has a seq. */
  reverts?: OpRef;
  /** "Revert to this point" (spec §6.4): sent as revertsToSeq, and never rebased. */
  revertsToSeq?: number;
}

/**
 * An entry that no longer applies, and why: an OpError, the ApiError of revertOutdated(), or
 * null when it undid a change that was itself dropped.
 */
export interface DroppedEntry {
  entry: PendingEntry;
  error: unknown;
}

export interface Replayed {
  tree: TreeState;
  kept: PendingEntry[];
  dropped: DroppedEntry[];
  /** The inverse of every kept entry on the replayed tree, by entry id. */
  inverses: Map<number, Op>;
}

/** Why a "Revert to this point" was dropped: someone saved a change after it was prepared. */
export function revertOutdated(): ApiError {
  return new ApiError('VERSION_CONFLICT', 'The family tree changed after the revert was prepared', 409);
}

/**
 * Applies the entries in order on `start` and drops those that no longer fit: they broke a
 * rule there, they undid a dropped change (`alreadyDropped` seeds those ids), or they revert to
 * a point while someone else saved a change (`othersSaved`).
 */
export function replayPending(
  start: TreeState,
  entries: readonly PendingEntry[],
  options: { alreadyDropped?: readonly number[]; othersSaved?: boolean } = {},
): Replayed {
  let tree = start;
  const kept: PendingEntry[] = [];
  const dropped: DroppedEntry[] = [];
  const inverses = new Map<number, Op>();
  const gone = new Set(options.alreadyDropped);
  for (const entry of entries) {
    let applied: ApplyOpResult | undefined;
    let error: unknown = null;
    if (entry.reverts && 'pendingId' in entry.reverts && gone.has(entry.reverts.pendingId)) {
      // It undid a change that was dropped: nothing is left to undo.
      error = null;
    } else if (entry.revertsToSeq !== undefined && options.othersSaved === true) {
      error = revertOutdated();
    } else {
      try {
        applied = applyOp(tree, entry.op);
      } catch (thrown) {
        if (!(thrown instanceof OpError)) throw thrown;
        error = thrown;
      }
    }
    if (applied) {
      tree = applied.tree;
      kept.push(entry);
      inverses.set(entry.id, applied.inverse);
    } else {
      gone.add(entry.id);
      dropped.push({ entry, error });
    }
  }
  return { tree, kept, dropped, inverses };
}

/** Pending entry `pendingId` was saved as `seq`: entries that revert it now name the seq. */
export function resolveRefs(
  entries: readonly PendingEntry[],
  pendingId: number,
  seq: number,
): PendingEntry[] {
  return entries.map((entry) =>
    entry.reverts && 'pendingId' in entry.reverts && entry.reverts.pendingId === pendingId
      ? { ...entry, reverts: { seq } }
      : entry,
  );
}

/** The request fields that link an entry to what it reverts (spec §6.1). */
export function revertFieldsOf(entry: PendingEntry): { revertsSeq?: number; revertsToSeq?: number } {
  if (entry.revertsToSeq !== undefined) return { revertsToSeq: entry.revertsToSeq };
  if (entry.reverts && 'seq' in entry.reverts) return { revertsSeq: entry.reverts.seq };
  return {};
}
```

Create `apps/web/src/features/tree/rebase.ts`:

```ts
import type { OperationEntry } from '@ancestors-tree/shared';
import { applyOp, OpError, sameOp, type Op, type TreeState } from '@ancestors-tree/shared/tree';
import { replayPending, resolveRefs, type DroppedEntry, type PendingEntry } from './pending';

export interface RebaseInput {
  confirmedTree: TreeState;
  confirmedVersion: number;
  pending: readonly PendingEntry[];
  /** The log after confirmedVersion, oldest first (GET /ops, every page). */
  remote: readonly OperationEntry[];
  /** The signed-in account: an op of its own in `remote` may be the queue's first. */
  userId: string;
}

/** The queue's first entry, found already saved at `seq`. */
export interface SavedEntry {
  entry: PendingEntry;
  seq: number;
}

export type RebaseResult =
  | { ok: false }
  | {
      ok: true;
      confirmedTree: TreeState;
      confirmedVersion: number;
      saved: SavedEntry | null;
      tree: TreeState;
      pending: PendingEntry[];
      dropped: DroppedEntry[];
      inverses: Map<number, Op>;
    };

/**
 * Spec §6.3: applies the missed ops to the confirmed tree, then replays the queue on top.
 * `ok: false` (reload the whole tree) when the log has a gap or a missed op does not apply to
 * this copy. When the server's first op by this account equals the queue's first entry, that
 * entry was saved before its answer was lost (a retry then got the 409): it is confirmed with
 * that seq instead of being sent twice.
 */
export function rebase(input: RebaseInput): RebaseResult {
  let confirmedTree = input.confirmedTree;
  let version = input.confirmedVersion;
  const head = input.pending[0];
  let saved: SavedEntry | null = null;
  let othersSaved = false;
  for (const remote of input.remote) {
    if (remote.seq !== version + 1) return { ok: false };
    try {
      confirmedTree = applyOp(confirmedTree, remote.op).tree;
    } catch (error) {
      if (error instanceof OpError) return { ok: false };
      throw error;
    }
    version = remote.seq;
    if (saved === null && head && remote.userId === input.userId && sameOp(remote.op, head.op)) {
      saved = { entry: head, seq: remote.seq };
    } else {
      othersSaved = true;
    }
  }
  const rest = saved ? resolveRefs(input.pending.slice(1), saved.entry.id, saved.seq) : [...input.pending];
  const replayed = replayPending(confirmedTree, rest, { othersSaved });
  return {
    ok: true,
    confirmedTree,
    confirmedVersion: version,
    saved,
    tree: replayed.tree,
    pending: replayed.kept,
    dropped: replayed.dropped,
    inverses: replayed.inverses,
  };
}
```

```ts
import {
  OPS_PAGE_SIZE,
  OperationListSchema,
  SubmitOpResponseSchema,
  TreeResponseSchema,
  type OperationEntry,
  type SubmitOpRequest,
  type SubmitOpResponse,
  type TreeResponse,
} from '@ancestors-tree/shared';
```

and add at the end:

```ts
/** Every operation saved after version `since`, oldest first, page after page. */
export async function fetchOpsSince(clanId: string, since: number): Promise<OperationEntry[]> {
  const entries: OperationEntry[] = [];
  let after = since;
  for (;;) {
    const page = await apiRequest(`/clans/${clanId}/ops?since=${after}`, OperationListSchema);
    entries.push(...page);
    const last = page.at(-1);
    if (page.length < OPS_PAGE_SIZE || last === undefined) return entries;
    after = last.seq;
  }
}
```

```ts
/**
 * Why a change was not saved: the broken rule (found by the server, or by the rebase on this
 * device), else the error code.
 */
export function rejectionMessage(t: TFunction, error: unknown): string {
  if (error instanceof OpError) return t(ruleKey(error.code));
  if (error instanceof ApiError && error.reason !== undefined) return t(ruleKey(error.reason));
  return t(errorMessageKey(error));
}
```
